ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u54	With this amendment, we want to go back to the abolition of special real estate privileges and their replacement by a legal mortgage. A legal mortgage is a security right in real property granted to the creditor by law, whereas the lien of a money lender is a security right that a lender may claim when granting a credit the amount of which is allocated to the acquisition of a real estate sale. We would like this last arrangement to be retained in the context of the introduction of a legal mortgage: yes to a legal mortgage if the lien of money lender remains.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u55	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u56	Unfavorable. The legal mortgage that will replace the lien of a money lender will not be more expensive than the lien.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u57	What is the Government's opinion?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u58	The purpose of the amendment is to change the scope of the authorization to reform the law of security rights by excluding the proposed deletion of the lien of money lenders. This deletion follows a logic of simplification and clarification of the law. However, the Government has noted the concern, conveyed in committee and in the Chamber, that the mortgage intended to replace the lien of money lenders entails an additional cost. We are obviously anxious to avoid such an extra cost. I repeat, the tax cost of the legal, and non-conventional, mortgage is strictly identical to that of the financial lender privilege. I therefore call for the amendment to be withdrawn; If I don't, I'll give you an unfavorable opinion.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u85	In France, there are two compulsory pension schemes by distribution: the basic and supplementary schemes, which vary according to whether one is a manager, an employee or an independent person. So everyone pays for everyone. There is also the supplementary pension scheme, which is optional: it is a funded system, in which one accumulates for oneself, and which passes through retirement savings products used mainly by senior managers. Through your bill, you want to expand the capitalization supplement by promoting tax reductions on these products. In reality, under the guise of financing the economy's financing products, it is a question of accompanying the future pension reform, on which one of the ways is to allow the highest incomes to no longer contribute to the distribution and to choose the capitalization. The targeted products are promoted by large, particularly active life insurance groups, says Mediapart, in Bercy's corridors.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u155	In paragraph 67, we would like to insert the words "in their retirement savings plans" after the words "financial". ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u156	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u157	What is the purpose of cantonment? To allocate the shareholdings to the profits, which are similar to the capital gains, and to secure the savings in the event of the default of the insurance company. We can go from a very fine cantonment, the one that you propose and that takes place at the scale of the plan, to a very wide cantonment, linked to the assets of the insurer. We have chosen an intermediate solution, placing the cantonment at the level of pension savings plans managed by the insurer. It seems to us the most appropriate, so I propose that you withdraw your amendment.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u167	What is the Government's opinion?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u168	Unfavorable opinion. There is a problem of market depth. As you know, the stock of life insurance today amounts to 1700 billion euros. Energy and environmental climate transition asset classes – TEEC – are proposed by only 22 funds and are mostly reserved for professional investors, for an outstanding amount of almost €4 billion. Solidarity funds are mainly corporate mutual funds – FCPE – that are not eligible for life insurance. These asset classes are still too limited to be massively offered in life insurance. We must create this market: the bill that you are going to pass today will boost the pace, but the offer must also be structured and of good quality for savers.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u169	I have before me two identical amendments, No 859 corrected and No 1121 corrected. Mrs Cendra Motin has the floor to support Amendment No 859 as corrected.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u170	This amendment, tabled by my colleague Bénédicte Peyrol, follows our discussions in the special committee. This is a withdrawal amendment, proposing that the proportion of the units of green, solidary and responsible accounts should be communicated to the persons concerned before the subscription. This obligation would allow subscribers to know the actual share of their savings that can be allocated to the ecological transition or the solidarity sector. This would encourage insurance companies to expand their catalogue of labelled offers. In line with the date of implementation of the new insurance dipping obligations, this amendment proposes an entry into force from 2022.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u171	I call Mr Potier to support Amendment No. 1121 as corrected.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u172	After discussions with The shift project, we made a proposal to facilitate the transfer of life insurance outstandings. This is a major means of action, as everyone agrees on the need for the public authority to mobilise around EUR 100 billion for the energy transition. For its part, the private sector could contribute EUR 10 billion. That is the meaning of the amendment we are proposing. The transfer of outstanding assets is more effective than subscription to the life insurance flow. But this very promising path faces one obstacle: the tax burden on these transfers. I know your reservations about the technical solution we are proposing. It is true that it is not fully completed – it could be clarified during the review of the finance bill. However, I would like to make suggestions which, if necessary, could be the subject of sub-amendments. One of the drawbacks of the idea of favouring the transfer of stocks is that this would weaken the long-term nature of life insurance investment. I am not a specialist of the latter, but I understood that his strength was linked to this long duration. But the transfer weakens the power of securities, thus their ability to finance the long-term energy transition. In order to improve our device, we could therefore only allow one transfer per operator. This would have an immediate leverage effect for large carriers, thanks to a defi-calization. The other solution, which is also dear to me, Mr Barrot, is to limit the duration of the operation to a few years in order to initiate a mobilization. Today, young people invited us to be bold: we could decide that subscribers can operate in 2019 and 2020. The burden that this provision would entail for the State would be offset by a "transfer shock" commensurate with the stakes. I do not know if our solution is technically sound, Mr rapporteur, but I absolutely want to engage in a solid conversation with you on this point.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u173	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u174	First of all, I would like to highlight the work done by Members of Parliament Bénédicte Peyrol, Dominique Potier, Matthieu Orphelin and others, who, together with you, the Secretary of State and the Government, have made great strides in trying to achieve considerable progress at first reading. For this new reading, Mr Potier has just presented his amendment, but also Amendment No 83. Insurers will have the obligation to present a socially responsible investment option – ISR –, a green option and a solidarity option, but also to display the percentage of green, solidarity and ISR funds they present to their customers. There will be sound competition between insurers, which will encourage them to take this route, in search of a form of labelling. On the amendment that you have just defended, Mr Potier, it turns out that we have had discussions on the possibility of transferring stocks that are in line with the ecological and solidarity transition. Indeed, by facilitating the transfer of life insurance contracts to new contracts – including those with the same insurer – which now include green, socially responsible or solidary options, subscribers are allowed to transition to contracts that match them better. We also voted to transfer a life insurance contract to the pension savings plan, which will also propose a solidarity option. The important work that has been done has led to significant gains, which will change the way the French approach life insurance, which remains their preferred savings product. There is much to bet that these provisions will initiate the pump of a greener and more solidary finance, going in the direction you want. I am issuing an opinion in favour of Amendments Nos 859 and 1121 as corrected. On the other hand, I would ask for Amendment No 83 to be withdrawn, in part satisfied with the text.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u175	What is the Government's opinion?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u176	On identical amendments Nos. 859 corrected and 1121 corrected, the Government gives a favourable opinion. I welcome the work done between the examination of the text in a special committee and its consideration in a public meeting. It has enabled a satisfactory way to communicate the proportion of funds directed towards the ecological, energy and solidarity transition within life insurance contracts, in order to avoid any effect of "greenwashing" or marketing, and to invite savers to question how to increase the proportion of such funds within their savings. As for Amendment No 83, of which Mr Potier has somewhat anticipated the defence, he raises a very important question. Thanks to the work done in the Special Committee to change the transferability of life insurance and to promote the development of competition, without calling into question the long-term investment horizon of insurance or destabilising its situation, we have managed to draft three amendments strengthening their transparency obligations. One of them facilitates the transfer of all or part of a life insurance contract to a retirement savings plan. Another expands the scope of the scheme allowing an insured person to transform a life insurance contract by amending or subscribing to a new contract, without changing an insurer, while retaining the benefit of the tax anteriority. This work seems to me to be of great value. Your amendment, Mr Potier, seems to me to be satisfied with this work. Partly, you'll tell me, considering maybe he's not going far enough. However, the mechanism you are proposing, given the low depth of the market, does not seem to us to be balanced as it stands. That is why I express an unfavorable opinion in advance.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u249	The rapporteur, Mr Jean-Noël Barrot, has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u250	Mr Mélenchon, we agree on one principle: the best way to finance the transition of our consumption and growth models is to rely on the savings of the French. In the reform of retirement savings, there is a measure that corresponds exactly to what you say: French people whose retirement savings will be placed in funds that invest enough in French SMEs will benefit from a reduced social package. So this idea already appears in the text – perhaps not with the scope you would like to give it. On the other hand, we disagree on the issue of debt. Of course, banks must play their part, and perhaps they are not sufficiently active and take enough risks on certain segments of the economy, particularly SMEs and in particular the cash flow of the economy.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u251	Not to mention craftsmanship!
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u252	However, there is nothing wrong, at times of the growth of some companies, with using other forms of financing, especially when projects are risky and innovative. The great explorations of the Renaissance and the discovery of the new world were not financed by debt, but by a form of participative lending that looked a little like equities... The same applies to innovation. Article 22 does not impose anything on any SME. However, for those who need it, it opens up the possibility of applying for such funding, when the risk of the project makes it necessary.

Indeed, the cost of an introduction to the stock exchange is between 5 and 7 % of the amount raised, which is very heavy. The European Commission is proposing a European regime that would allow, inter alia, the introduction of a new definition of bond issuers and the facilitation of the transition from an SME growth market to a regulated market. The aim of this appeal amendment is therefore to press for the European initiative to succeed quickly.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u336	A few months ago, the Autorité des marchés financiers changed its jurisprudence and prohibited cooperative companies from offering their capital securities to the public. This decision has had a strong impact on social and solidarity economy enterprises, which, like their cousins of the so-called classic economy, need equity and investment to grow. To do so, they must be able to access financing opportunities, especially if the lucrativity of these companies is null or limited. The co-operative model is based on the opportunity for as many people as possible to participate in governance. The prohibition puts cooperatives at a disadvantage compared to non-cooperative public limited liability companies, without any justification other than a different denomination of capital shares. Our honourable senators have responded to this problem, which obviously goes in the right direction but remains partial, by adopting an amendment allowing cooperative societies of collective interest formed in the form of public limited companies to make offers to the public of financial securities. The amendment complements this by allowing all cooperative companies, provided they are formed in the form of public limited companies, to make such offers to the public.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u376	The defence of this amendment will require great attention. We have worked hard to make it understandable and exciting for those who attend this great evening. It is gratifying that this bill is seriously addressing the issue. However, is the answer now up to the problem? We're not quite sure, because looking at some cryptocurrencies, we have something to worry about if a crash happens. To take just one example, a bitcoin is now worth 3150 euros; A year ago, it was worth 14810. From day to day, it is all investors' savings that could go into dematerialized smoke, causing a significant part of the economy to fall. In view of the stakes, publishing codes of conduct and encouraging the AMF to award quality labels to virtuous actors in the sector is a necessity. Similarly, the creation of an accreditation regime involving the CPRA – everyone knows what this acronym means – and the prohibition of online advertising by providers who have not obtained the approval or the optional visa is in the right direction. However, we wonder why the visa is only optional. The Government justifies its position by referring to the free will of economic actors and by relying on the quality label awarded by the AMF. In concrete terms, we are experimenting with the hope that, with good communication, savers will turn to the actors targeted by the AMF. Anyone who regularly browses the Internet knows that investment incentives are so pressing and information so hard to get that there is a lot to bet that sorting between the right and the wrong grain will not be easy. That is why, in view of the issues at stake, we believe it is essential to strengthen the control of the AMF by introducing a compulsory visa. In the light of the forthcoming FATF publication, it seems necessary to be able to revisit the subject, in particular on the scope of the coverage of service providers in the sector, which may not be fully compliant with FATF standards when adopted. I recall that a regulation of crypto-actives in the fight against money laundering and the financing of terrorism was one of the key issues for France at the conference "No Money For Terror", led by the President of the Republic, held in Paris in April 2018. The resulting Paris agenda, which all countries committed themselves to respecting, was clear: "We are committed to applying FATF standards with respect to crypto-actives. Given the strong involvement of France and our President in this conference, we will need to be particularly careful to take into account what the FATF will specify before June. That is the position which I supported in the special committee and which I wanted to recall.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u531	The aim is to condition the eligibility of the shares of FCPI – the joint investment fund in innovation – to the PEA-SME with the investment quota in European SME-ETI securities. Article 27b of this draft law provides in particular for the extension of the scope of employment eligible for the PEA-SME to include shares of professional private equity funds – FPCI. The shares of such funds, which take the form either of mutual funds or of investment companies with variable capital – SICAV – are already eligible for the PEA-SME. They must comply with the conditions of ordinary law, in particular the requirement that their assets be constituted for at least 75 % of SME-ETI securities, two thirds of which must be securities eligible for direct investment. The measure adopted at first reading leads to the full eligibility of the shares of these FCPIs to the PEA-SME, which would then be exempt from compliance with the PEA-SME common law quota. However, it does not appear justified to exempt these FCPIs, which are funds reserved for professional investors, from compliance with investment quotas, at the risk of distorting the very purpose of the PEA-SME. As a result, the previous amendment aims to reverse this measure by conditioning the eligibility of FCPI's shares in the PEA-SME to the above mentioned European SME-ETI investment quota.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u532	The rapporteur, Mr Jean-Noël Barrot, has the floor to support Amendment No. 1234.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u533	It's editorial.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u534	I call Mrs Nadia Hai to support Amendment No. 147.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u535	The purpose of this amendment is to revert to the wording of the article as adopted at first reading by the House, which retains the necessary protections for companies against shadow banking, among other things, while relaxing the arrangements. In order for our citizens to understand what this is all about, inter-firm loans, which are relatively common in our economy, can lead to risks, particularly for companies that abuse these loans. Therefore, in order to protect against such risks, it is important to maintain the condition of the economic link between the undertakings involved in this lending relationship. The removal, voted by the Senate, of the economic link condition between the lending firm and the company receiving the loan would therefore create a risk situation for companies, especially for the smaller ones, by exposing them to acquisition and diversification strategies led by other actors. The development of shadow finance, or the persistence of inter-firm payment deadlines, calls for the preservation of the economic link. I therefore request the deletion of paragraph 5.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u553	I call Mr Dharréville to support Amendment No. 341 to delete the Rule.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u554	It is a question of developing preference actions in this article, which we believe will increase the dependence of small and medium-sized enterprises and medium-sized enterprises on financial markets. Preferential actions, which are in fact actions with special rights, were introduced in French law by the Order of 24 June 2004, itself inspired by foreign legislation, in particular those of Anglo-Saxon countries. They allow, among other things, minority shareholders who have strictly financial objectives and do not wish to become involved in the management of the company, to adjust their priority financial rights to benefit from a priority dividend or a preferential distribution of the price in the event of a division of the company. In addition, in unlisted companies, preference shares are particularly targeted at so-called passing shareholders, for whom the acquisition of an interest is by nature temporary. We are therefore hostile to this logic of tenant capitalism.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u555	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u556	Unfavorable opinion. That being said, it can be said that the Senate had relaxed the conditions for issuing and redeeming preference shares, and it seemed to us more prudent to return, for the most part, to the drafting of the Assembly.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u568	I would like to highlight the Senate's progress on reverse factoring, a provision added at first reading by an amendment by President Patrick Mignola and several of our colleagues in the MODEM and related groups. This provision follows the principle of factoring, i.e. the management of accounts receivable by an external provider specialised in recovery and litigation, but by inverting it. Thus, unlike conventional factoring, reverse factoring is established by the customer and not by the supplier. This helps to reduce delays in payment times. The Senate wanted to extend this principle, initially reserved for public procurement, to all buyers, while simplifying the procedure. This is a good initiative, especially for SMEs. In order to further reduce late payments, I call on the Government to consider a revision of the New Economic Regulations Act 2001, in order to shorten payment deadlines and, in particular, to abolish the regulation to 45 days at the end of the month. French specificity, which does not exist in any other European State, this derogation period, which must be included in the contract, is a source of complexity. Removing it would simplify, help to shorten payment deadlines and make harmonisation desirable at European level. I hope, Madam Secretary of State, that you will be able to take the necessary steps to continue to simplify, harmonise and defend our SMEs.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u641	The Commission has deleted Rule 42a A.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u642	Mr Philippe Bolo has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u643	To feed the debate on Article 42 bis, here are various elements from the important work done by my excellent colleague Sylvain Waserman, coming from 17 meetings he has organized since February 2018, with institutional, economic, rapporteur and government actors, on the issue of strong patents. Currently, the examination of patent applications by INPI – the national institute of intellectual property – leads to quasi-automatic grant, resulting in a presumption of weak validity of French patents and, consequently, a significant risk to their holders. Thus, an SME owner who finds himself in court to defend his rights to a French patent has a high probability that it is simply overturned by the judge. Strengthening substantive examination of patent applications will improve confidence in the French system. If our country wants to compete with China, Germany or Japan, it must, like them, switch to a strong patent system. Some amendments provide for the possibility for the applicant to choose between a strong patent and a weak patent to remedy this situation, but such a development would lead to the creation of a two-tier system for patent holders. I would remind you that there is already a certificate of utility which the PACTE law will strengthen: companies will therefore have the possibility to choose between this certificate and the strong patent. It seems to me important to make it clear that the strong patent application will not entail any additional costs, since the INPI already subcontracts to the European Patent Office the completion of the search report. Companies are awaiting reform of patent legislation. Several dozen of them, as well as leading figures in the intellectual property world, have signed letters of public support for the implementation of the strong patent. Conversely – I would point out for the record – the lobbying firm that works for opposition to the strong patent was able to give only three corporate contacts by being part of it, the other contacts being confidential. It is therefore time to finally take the step and to bring France into the system of strong patents. That is why the Democratic and Related Movement will support the rapporteur's position and will not vote for the amendments to revert to this mechanism.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u649	First, this section is contrary to the simplification objective of the bill; Second, its application will entail additional costs, estimated at more than 20%, which will amount to a few thousand euros for each patent grant; Third, review times will be much longer, resulting in some inertia in economic activity. For all these reasons, we propose to delete it.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u650	What is the committee's opinion on these deletion amendments?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u651	At first reading, we had discussed this article at some length, the provisions of which represent a real revolution in French patent law. I would also like to pay tribute to the work done by our colleague Sylvain Waserman. The current patent affects the credibility of French innovation because the only verified criterion for the grant of the title is novelty and not at all inventive step. We therefore have the least robust patents in Europe, 40% to 44% of them being cancelled in the event of a dispute, compared to only 23% in our neighbours. In addition, the current system tends to favour large firms when they compete with SMEs. The legal uncertainty of the new procedure is often mentioned, but this is not justified. It is rather today that insecurity prevails, as an SME that has filed a patent has no certainty that it is unattainable. While it had to spend relatively large amounts to obtain it, it did not have a guarantee on the quality of the issued title. Our objective is therefore to strengthen the attractiveness of the French patent by aligning itself with major innovative nations such as Germany, the United States, Japan, South Korea and China. Of course, as some companies have pointed out, Spain tends to go back in this area. But I would remind you that Germany is still considered to be a particularly innovative nation and it is therefore more appropriate to align itself with its model. There is also a whole discussion on the complementarity between the national patent and the European patent: they can be complementary while both are strong. We heard a lot: "If you make a strong patent in France, we will make a deposit at the European level. But we are not against the European patent: we are in favour of a strong French patent, even if it would look very much like its European counterpart. I would remind you that it will always be possible for a company to choose only to file a patent at European level, even if it is then extended at national level. The fear of greater complexity and risk of additional costs was also raised. It does not seem relevant to us since INPI is already subcontracting to the European Patent Office the production of the search report, which provides information on inventive step. Since the report is heavily subsidized by INPI, the new procedure will not weigh more on companies than the previous one. I will conclude by pointing out that Mrs.Lamure, rapporteur in the Senate, is also in favour of this mechanism.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u652	What is the Government's opinion?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u653	I welcome the work of Parliament on this important issue, which has benefited from strong involvement not only from Members of Parliament – including the MODEM – but also from senators. I would also like to point out that the proposed arrangement has been the subject of wide-ranging consultation. The result seems likely to give our country time to advance in an area where we were fragile, as the rapporteur pointed out very well. For all these reasons, I think it would be a shame to delete the article. Unfavorable opinion.

It is not the examination prior to its grant that confers the force of a patent; On the contrary, it can reveal its weakness. PACTE is supposed to reduce costs for businesses while this article will impose an additional budget on them, with consequences for their competitiveness. It is therefore reasonable to allow the applicant the opportunity to make a request for substantive examination when he considers it appropriate to strengthen the credibility of his patent application. The purpose of this amendment is to make it optional for INPI to examine inventive step.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u694	Mr Guillaume Kasbarian has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u695	I will try to be short – and simple – because it is late. We will address an important part of the bill, which deals with foreign investment in France and, more generally, with the economic security of our country, a subject to which we are all sensitive. In the vast majority of cases, foreign investment in France is going very well, and France is even delighted to welcome new investors. But we must not be naive, because we are living an economic war. Not all investors are necessarily well-intentioned. In addition, these investments can lead to the growth of our economy going under the control of other countries at the risk of creating a national trauma: I am thinking of Alstom, STX France and Alcatel. Finally, industrial espionage scandals erupt regularly. For example, a Chinese telecommunications company is currently being prosecuted in the United States in a case involving theft of technology on a robot model. This is the context in which Articles 55 et seq. come into play. Our Assembly is very attentive to the issues of economic security and foreign investment in France. A commission of inquiry, requested by the Republican Group and chaired by Olivier Marleix, was set up to examine "State decisions on industrial policy". Bringing together some 30 Members of Parliament from all walks of life, she sat for six months and made a number of recommendations at the end of her work aimed at securing foreign investment in France and improving the economic security of our country. On the other hand, the Government is also very much in favour of better supervision of foreign investment in France. Bruno Le Maire has thus revised the decree on foreign investment in France subject to prior authorisation – IEF – to improve the device and extend it to new technologies, new know-how and new cases of use. A reform of the Strategic Information Service is also expected. On the legislative front, article 55 aims to ensure that the public authority can ensure effective control of foreign investment: a strong sanctioning power is granted to the Minister of the Economy, and a regularisation procedure is provided for in order not to block projects. The National Assembly and the Government have therefore worked together to improve the control of foreign investment in France. However, it is a point on which, despite long months of discussion, consensus is complicated to find: that of parliamentary scrutiny. The conclusions of the report of the Committee of Inquiry included the creation of a parliamentary delegation for economic security. Our group, like all the others, was in favour of this, so that at first reading an amendment to that effect was adopted unanimously, which we were very pleased with. However, at first reading, the Senate decided to delete this provision, a decision that my colleagues and I deeply regret. The High Assembly felt that economic security was not a major issue justifying the creation of a new delegation, that parliamentary scrutiny was sufficient, and that it was therefore unnecessary to strengthen Parliament's powers in this area. For its part, our Assembly is committed to the idea of creating a parliamentary delegation to economic security. Last week, in committee, we reintroduced this provision, which, I would remind you, was adopted unanimously at first reading. The problem is that the Senate cannot be forced to work on the matter if it does not wish. It therefore seems complicated, in these circumstances, to establish a joint delegation to the two chambers. That is why we will propose an alternative mechanism that has been the subject of lengthy discussions with the Government, the rapporteurs and the various groups. It is important to me, and I will conclude on this point, that we reach a broad consensus in the Chamber: indeed, on such matters the wars of chapels or ego have no meaning. [[Applause on several benches of the LaREM group. – The president of the special committee, the rapporteur-general and the rapporteur also applaud]] Our goal is to better protect our industrial flowers and the jobs they represent against foreign investors. As far as economic security is concerned, the danger does not come from the inside, but from the outside. We must therefore achieve national unity. We reached a consensus at first reading as well as in committee; I am convinced that we will be able to achieve this again at the end of a calm debate. We owe it to investors, businesses and, of course, employees in our industry.

It is therefore urgent to take protective measures. The deregulation movements we have been denouncing for years are therefore accelerating. The very announcement of a future law, which will take six months, one year or even two years, requires the adoption of emergency protection measures, which could take the form of an extension of the scope of the devices that we are going to adopt.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u762	Defendant.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u763	The Secretary of State has the floor to support sub-amendment No. 1337.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u764	Amendment No 1268 gives Parliament significant powers of control and investigation. It excludes investment authorisation procedures under investigation, but includes authorisation decisions for which conditions have been imposed on the investor. However, compliance with those conditions was monitored by the Government, which could decide to impose sanctions if it found faults. This amendment would undermine the constitutional principle of separation of powers, as it would allow the legislative power to interfere in proceedings still under way, and therefore influence individual decisions. This would also undermine France's attractiveness to international investors. The purpose of this sub-amendment is therefore to limit room and on-site control to matters for which the Minister of the Economy will no longer have to make a decision. In doing so, it is fully part of the mandate of monitoring public policies entrusted to Parliament by the Constitution.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u765	What is the opinion of the committee on all these sub-amendments?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u766	I will respond in a comprehensive manner on this subject before giving the opinion of the committee. I hear your frustration, Mr. Fasquelle. This would be a first step towards a more lasting inclusion of this control which, for the time being, can only be exercised through commissions of inquiry established in case of suspicion. We want to work together to demystify this procedure, to inform the French, to reassure them of the actual existence of control over strategic investments, led by competent services, and to tell them that we do not brace our strategic sectors. That is what I wanted to tell you about the intermediate solution we found. The opinion is in favour of sub-amendments Nos. 1332 corrected, 1333 corrected and 1337, and against all other sub-amendments.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u771	What is the Government's opinion on the amendment and the other sub-amendments?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u772	As I have indicated, the proposed arrangement significantly strengthens Parliament's control power over economic security. The Government is, of course, prepared to transmit all available information to parliamentarians appointed to exercise this control in a climate of confidence. This is the purpose of the annual report mentioned in the amendment, but also of the hearings. However, caution is exercised in maintaining the confidentiality and smooth conduct of operations under the responsibility of the Minister of Economy and Finance. Your amendment gives the chairmen of the Committee on Economic Affairs and the general rapporteurs of the Finance Committees of the two chambers significant powers of control and investigation, particularly with regard to foreign investment in France. It excludes investment authorisation procedures under investigation, which is in the right direction, but includes authorisation decisions for which the conditions have been imposed on the investor. That is why we propose, through a sub-amendment, a correction to ensure the proper division of roles between the executive and the legislature. I therefore propose that you supervise the exercise of control on the spot and on the spot by limiting it to matters for which the Minister of the Economy will no longer have to make a decision. On the other hand, if, as I ask, the cases on which the executive can still take decisions are excluded, the committee chairmen will still have access to simple authorizations, refusals and authorizations with conditions when they no longer exist. Parliamentary oversight of the implementation of public policies must be based on an ex post evaluation of the Government's action. I have an opinion of wisdom on Amendment No 1268, which organises the work of Parliament, and it is therefore up to it to decide. Unfavourable opinion on the sub-amendments relating to part and on-the-spot checks and wisdom opinions, for the same reasons as before, on those relating to the quality of the members having access to the file – namely the corrected sub-amendments No 1332, 1333 corrected, 1335 1328 and 1310 – even if the corrected sub-amendments No 1332 and 1333 corrected seem to us to have the best balance.

Such a procedure does not exist anywhere else: in the United States, members of the Congress who sit on the CFIUS – Committee on foreign investment in the United States – do not control the Government over ongoing investments. I therefore fully understand the principle. Nevertheless, I repeat, the amendment refers to "investments that may be the subject of decisions by the minister". For Alstom, last year, with Mr Marleix, we carried out a piece and on-site check. Alstom was a company "likely" to be the subject of a decision by the Minister – since it was still in the commitment period – including a sanction, for example for failing to comply with the obligation to create jobs. So I just want to make sure that the wording here does not imply a global ban. I am well aware, Madam Secretary of State, that you and the Minister of the Economy are fully prepared to share the information with us, but in the future, could the word "likely" not justify, for some, blocking any control? ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u852	I will present it in conjunction with Amendment No 1242, which is a variant of it. We propose to return to participation. Apart from wages, there are two ways for employees to receive remuneration: participation and interest. In any event, we prefer that wages be as high as possible. Otherwise, we prefer participation in interest because it preserves the inclusion of the return of added value in a logic of law, rather than in a logic of privileges. Its formula of calculation dates from the Gaullist era, characterized on this point by the desire to promote the sharing of profits with employees.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u853	All right!
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u854	The formula developed at the time, we all know it: it is the product of half the value of tax income minus 5% of equity, and of the wage bill-to-value-added ratio. At the time of General de Gaulle, corporate taxation accounted for about 50% of value added. Today, it is less than 30% and is part of a downward trajectory, even though there is a very strong will to restore profit sharing, after three decades of great distortion that have seen capital – hence shareholders – capture most of the added value. For all these reasons, we propose to abolish the two-fold division provided for in the formula in force, which would be almost equivalent, in the case of taxation, to the restoration of the product of the formula obtained with the figures of the Gaullian era. In other words, we propose updating the formula for calculating participation. You will answer, Madam Secretary of State, that complex work is under way, within the framework of a collegiality designed to discern the debates of the same order and to open them – one can take them for several years! It seems to me that Parliament could, to a wise and prudent extent, adopt the proposal that we are making here, which is a measure of justice, and allow the social partners, if necessary, to agree on alternative formulas, more detailed or more precise depending on the areas. It is not up to us to delegate everything, but it is up to us to take over the crucial issue of sharing added value.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u855	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u856	I, too, will speak on both amendments. This topic has been discussed at length, both in our hearings and in the consultation organized by the Government. It is true that the current participation formula may raise questions for some structures. However, consultation with the social partners has not led to any consensus on a new formula. We must not abandon this construction site; I even think we need to pursue it. The Government has recently appointed two wage savings ambassadors. Their mission is to examine this issue as well, which could lead to putting back on the table the construction site inaugurated by the COPIESAS – guidance board for participation, interest, wage savings and employee share ownership – which I would remind you has not been successful, as the diversity of structures has prevented finding a more favourable formula than the current one. However, if a given structure does not find it at its convenience, it is always possible for it to be better-known, which is in the interests of the employees. Unfavorable opinion.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u857	Mrs Laure de La Raudière has the floor to support Amendment No. 538.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u858	Paragraph 49 of this section, which provides for the capping by order of account fees applicable to salary savings plans, is welcome. The amendment is intended to regulate a little more the consultation of the social actors which you are going to carry out, Madam Secretary of State, by ensuring that the ceilings cannot exceed the financial product of the investment. Account must be taken of the fact that an employee leaving a company may wish to retain the benefit of the wage savings plans to which he or she has subscribed. Sometimes he has to. As a result, he pays account fees, especially if he does not join a new company to transfer his salary savings plans. It should therefore be made clear that the account-keeping costs provided for in paragraph 49 may not exceed the annual financial proceeds of the related investment, so that the retention of the wage savings account does not affect the capital saved.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u859	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u860	This provision was adopted by the Senate and deleted last week in a special committee, for a simple reason: if a salary savings plan had very negative performance for a year, it would result from the adoption of your amendment that it would be subject to a negative ceiling. As a result, the plan manager would not receive any pay for keeping the account, which we believe is a problem. On the other hand, I would point out that we have adopted several guarantees in Article 58 in this respect, ensuring transparency of management costs. Thus, it is now possible to determine whether they are excessive or not.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u888	The purpose of the article is to encourage employee ownership in enterprises. There are two main reasons for our opposition. The first is the risk of substitution for wages caused by the development of employee ownership. Due to the tax incentives adopted in 2015 under the Macron law, companies will certainly be tempted to facilitate delayed and random remuneration of employees, to the detriment of a general increase in wages. In this connection, it is worth recalling that last autumn the majority voted in the Social Security Financing Act to reduce the tax on free shares. This measure also contributes to a circumvention of the socialised wage. We are far away, you will know, from the cooperative! The second reason why we oppose this article is that we fear that employees will thus become investors in their own company, but not be fully integrated into its decision-making bodies: it would place them in the hamster's position in a wheel. This mechanism puts employees at risk of losing everything at once: their employment and their savings. In our view, it is a great illusion that you propose to them.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u889	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u890	As with participation earlier, I hear your concern: you fear a substitution effect to pay. It is not this, and here our logics are opposed: we believe, on the contrary, that by involving more, as shareholders, the employees of a company, they will participate not only in the capital but also in the vision, the strategy of the company. This is fundamental to the concept of social responsibility, which we will talk about earlier, which integrates all the organic issues of a company's life and where they take a prominent place. You will, moreover, certainly be happy to vote with us for the restoration of a provision which we adopted at first reading, and which strengthens the weight, the voice of the shareholders employed in the FCPE. They thus keep control of what becomes of their capital. Finally, if you want to follow the consequences of this extension of employee ownership, the rapporteur-general and I propose that you amend the overall assessment of the law provided for in Article 74. We undertake to accept your proposal on this point. I propose that you withdraw the amendment; Failing that, adverse notice.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u906	This is an amendment of legal precision, adopted by the Special Committee. We reinstated the provision that we adopted at first reading, which was amended by the Senate. This article modifies the conduct of voting operations in employee share ownership FCPEs – it answers questions about the real weight of employee ownership, Mr Dharréville. To date, the supervisory boards of the CFPE have included shareholders, representatives of the company and representatives by trade union designation. At first reading, we voted for the dismissal in the vote of the company's management when it comes to their own company, considering that there was a conflict of interest. The Senate restored the position of the leader for its own deliberations, and removed the designation by the union organizations in the representation of the CFPEs, seeing it as another conflict of interest. Between the first and second readings, we had many discussions with trade union organisations, as well as with representatives of employee shareholders and employers. We are leading to a new solution, although it seems obvious: we allow the shareholders, and only they, who bear shares, to make decisions in the FCPEs. In order to do so, we do not restore the designation of the trade union organizations, which agree, provided that we restore what we had adopted at first reading, that is, the dismissal of the head of the enterprise. He may be there to deliberate, but not during voting. This, I think, is really an amendment of fairness.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u913	Mr Dominique Potier has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u914	A few words from the general philosophy of this article, which amends the civil code, following the work on enterprise reform carried out at the Collège des Bernardins. This work also included a section on codetermination – we will return to it. I know the controversy that this is going to cause. On the one hand, on the right, there are those who consider that the reform of the civil code creates a legal risk for businesses – in my view, not only does this objection not hold in legal terms, but this amendment will be symbolically very strong, and will inspire other laws. On the other hand, this time that of the Members of the La République Group on the move, it is in the sense of the words that we will focus on: if we reform the civil code, we must reform it for real. But almost all the academics who have worked on the subject insist on the distinction between "take into account" and "take into account" – this is not the opinion of Nicole Notat and Dominique Senard, but their word is not gospel. For our part, we are on the side of academics who have worked on the meaning of words: the company must, they say, take into account the economic, social and environmental consequences of its actions. The revolution of the civil code presupposes this semantic precision, which we will therefore ask. There will also be a discussion of the raison d'être of companies. We will finally agree with your proposal. But Boris Vallaud and I were lucky enough to be received by Mrs. Notat and Mr. Senard. This one told us to have an idea: to fix a distant star, a trajectory. Thus, the company he was still chairing recently would no longer be intended to manufacture tyres, but to prepare for the sustainable mobility of the 21st century... Hear me well, I say this without irony! But saying words does not magically change the management of the box, the sharing of added value or the relationships with its partners around the world. It is a declaration of intent, and in my view it is an added value. This faculty seems to me happy, but I call for a little restraint: let us not make it the alibi of a revolution that would be only verbal. Yes to the raison d'être, yes to the precision of the civil code, but let's talk more about codetermination: if it is the declaration of the shareholder pact that says the raison d'être and sets the consideration of the social and environmental consequences of the company's action, then we will have made a little mockery of the world.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u915	Mr Daniel Fasquelle has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u916	Here we come to a hard point in the text, one of those on which we oppose. What you are proposing is, I think, extremely dangerous for companies. So you want to include in the civil code that "society is managed in its social interest, taking into account the social and environmental stakes of its activity". But when can we decide that the company has fulfilled this obligation? A company must respect environmental law, labour law... But with this new provision, it will have to do more than that. When will it have done better than respecting the environmental code? When will she have done better than abide by the labour code? Where will the limit be placed, and how will we know? I'm asking the question, but I don't see how you're going to be able to answer it... Second question, who will decide whether this obligation has been met? As soon as you create a new obligation – don't smile, Mr. Lescure, the case is serious – it is necessary, at one point or another, that his non-compliance be punished. The judge will therefore have to intervene. Mr. Lescure, you may be very strong in re-establishing the Caisse de dépôt et placement du Québec, but to each of you, your domain. For my part, I have considered and studied the matter well. At some point, therefore, someone will have to decide whether the company has complied with its obligation to take into account its social and environmental issues. This will necessarily be the judge, to whom you will give a little more power. Third question, who's going to go to the judge? Not only the company's stakeholders, but also those outside the company. These could be environmental protection associations – we know of serious and far less serious ones – that would call into question any company that would not have acted in consideration of the social and environmental issues of its business. And even if there was only a small probability that what I have just explained will happen – you can't completely rule it out – it is criminal to want our country's companies to take this risk.

This article is the cornerstone of this new way of building businesses. We are trying, not to impose on entrepreneurs what could be a danger, since organizations could blame companies for not being careful enough, but for recognizing in the law the break that I have just mentioned. I can sum up in a simple way this new way of thinking: during the period of traditional capitalism, we were looking for maximization – profits, power relations, returns on investment. I propose that we go towards optimisation, i.e. taking into account common interests, which will certainly allow us to write a good page of future economic history.

We're even beginning to be able to quantify them. Social and environmental responsibility rests on different pillars. Of the seven core CSR issues, two have already been encrypted. When a structure develops CSR policy in its relations with external stakeholders, its overall productivity and performance gains are around 5.6%. When CSR policies target internal stakeholders, i.e. working conditions, overall performance gains are around 20%. It's huge! It's good for the employee, for the company, for our economy, for society, for the consumer and for the citizen. Why are we stopping? Let us move forward collectively on this subject! ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1016	If we refer to what Mr Lescure explained to us earlier, it is almost revolutionary to think that a company does not have as its sole objective the interests of its associates. In this area, the development introduced by the draft law on the status of enterprises is in the right direction. For the employees of the former and the latter, everything is going worse and worse because of an unprecedented aggressive management, since companies know only one stallion: capitalist rent. However, since your proposals have to be taken seriously, we propose an amendment to include the fact that the company, far from being reducible to the interests of shareholders, is above all a human collective, that it reflects a humanistic vision and has a social object. The first people whose interests it must defend are those who, if they stopped working, would prevent it from existing: its employees, who produce its wealth, or its subcontractors, often pressurized. According to Mr. Lescue, the article would allow a more virtuous principle to be exercised despite competition between companies. I register falsely against this idea. In all the laws you have passed, the so-called social one represents the standard adopted by all companies, including those who initially wanted to be virtuous. [[Applause on the benches of the FI group]] We propose that workers, subcontractors, users and shareholders be taken into account not only in the interests of the company, but more broadly in an ecological and social dimension respecting the environment, as well as the social and territorial balance of the human community in which the company is inserted.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1017	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1018	Unfavorable opinion. I see what this argument is about, but the wording of the amendment has no very strong legal scope. It reflects a respectable political position, like any political position, but which could not be included in the civil code, except by destabilizing the architecture of this code and, more generally, commercial law. As regards the more specific relationship between the subcontractor and the payer, the article is more protective than the current law. In order to prepare the text, we interviewed representatives of subcontractors from large companies. These SME leaders have embarked on social and environmental policies under corporate social responsibility. When a principal does not adopt an environmental policy, he or she may be forced to do so economically. Thanks to the provisions that we introduce, the subcontractor will be able to assert this, which will allow it to rebalance certain relations. You want to take care of subcontractors, regardless of the size of their business, and put everyone on an equal footing to launch social and environmental policies? In this regard, the article is excellent.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1019	What is the Government's opinion?

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1071	The article is interesting, in the sense that it demands and allows companies to respect certain social and environmental advances. However, some points need to be clarified and companies should also be called upon to combat discrimination, which is a real scourge in the professional world. Thus, immigrants or descendants of immigrants suffer from genuine discrimination in hiring and are twice as exposed to unemployment as other employees. According to the last report of the association SOS homophobia, 11% of LGBTphobic acts take place in the professional community, 49% of them by colleagues and 29% by supervisors. In France, the income of women is 24 per cent lower than that of men and, in an equivalent position, there is a pure discrimination of 10 per cent, even though, according to all studies, women are on average more educated than men. [[Applause on the benches of the FI group]] These discriminations are a real scourge and seriously undermine equality in our country. It is our duty as legislator to impose a duty on companies to combat such discrimination. That is why we propose, by this amendment, to make this struggle one of the objectives of companies.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1072	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1073	This is an important cause, which is the subject of very successful legislation in France. The Labour Code sets out twenty-two criteria for discrimination in the world of work, recruitment, career development and termination of work. This is a cause for which I have been engaged for a very long time – it started long before my political life began. I have deepened my knowledge of the subject, read the reports of the Defender of Rights, met with professors of national and international law and sociologists. These people explain that most illegal corporate discrimination – I will not address other forms of discrimination – is often due to unconscious cognitive biases. Therefore, what works best to put an end to discrimination, including hiring, is to go to companies to raise awareness, create recruitment tools and processes – it's very effective. These mechanisms are provided for in the Labour Code and in managerial policies on diversity, which eliminate all unconscious cognitive biases. Discriminatory, conscious decisions, which are extremely serious, are sanctioned by both the Labour Code and the Penal Code. Moreover, under French law, under European influence, we have gone further in the fight against discrimination. This is one of the only areas in which the burden of proof has been reversed, in order to facilitate the prosecution of victims. The current system seems complete to me, and I thank Europe for that. As a first step, France had fallen behind, particularly in transposing a European directive. Europe has pushed us towards equal pay for women and men and has made it possible to adopt the directive on combating different forms of discrimination. It took us a long time to transpose it, but we did. At present, it must be maintained in the courts, in businesses and through awareness-raising. On the other hand, the mechanism you are proposing is less powerful, less concrete than the existing standards, and would not be beneficial to the fight against discrimination. The opinion is therefore unfavourable.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1102	[[Applause on the benches of the FI group]] We want to strengthen equality in the sharing of wealth produced by employees. Recall that between 2009 and 2016, for 100 euros of profits, the CAC40 companies on average donated 67.4 euros of dividends to shareholders, leaving only 27.3 euros for reinvestment and 5.3 euros for bonuses for employees. Whatever our political positions – or perhaps not – we all can and do – or perhaps not – by showing common sense, that such a distribution is perfectly illegitimate in terms of stakeholder involvement. For many members of the majority, taking risks for the smooth running of a business is considered necessary and is seen as a criterion of the value of entrepreneurs. For our part, we believe that the same can be said of employees. Workers take risks for the smooth running of their business. It is not their shareholders but they who may be victims of accidents at work – who kill employees every day – or occupational diseases. They can also lose their jobs. Without them, no production is possible. This inequitable sharing therefore does not respond to any rationally estimated logic. It is only a reflection of a balance of forces: in the 1970s and 1980s, employees were better organized and better represented, and profits were better distributed. After 30 years of liberal policies, reduction of trade unionism, break-up of labour law and mass unemployment, the place of employees was reduced to the congruous portion. But it is up to the law to restore balance, where force has replaced the principle of justice. That is why we are asking you to adopt this amendment, which makes the definition of a company subject to the obligation to distribute at least half of the profits to employees.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1146	We propose to take up the provision adopted by the House which provides for the creation of a label for companies which justify the introduction of a policy of accessibility and inclusion of disabled people in ordinary environments. On 25 July last in the Assembly, the Secretary of State to the Prime Minister, in charge of disabled people, Sophie Cluzel, embarked on a process of better integration of disability throughout the life course of these people. This action therefore requires a better inclusion of persons with disabilities in businesses in the ordinary environment. At a time when their definition is going to mutate in order to better take into account social and environmental objects, it seems essential to include the fight against discrimination based on disability in the world of work. This will for action is based on a simple mathematical observation: people with disabilities are twice as affected by unemployment as the rest of the population. Indeed, being disabled in France means having three times less chances of being employed, twice as likely to be unemployed and to have a longer job search period of at least one year. The economic actors as well as those specializing in disability are already working on this idea of a label following the first reading of this draft law on the growth and transformation of the enterprise. The company's stakeholders – employers as social partners – are prepared not only to take part in its elaboration, but above all to carry out any measures that can enhance their actions to promote the inclusion of people with disabilities, considering that disability is an opportunity and not a burden for the company. Let's create this label!

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1153	Mr Dominique Potier has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1154	I would like to stress how much the Socialist and related group is interested in the idea of a public label for CSR. A few words of history. CSR was not born with this text, the oldest traces of it in the 19th century are found in societies of philanthropy and, more concretely, in law, in 1992 following the Rio summit. Another year to remember is 2012, after the subprime crisis: a European directive requires extra-financial reporting. It is at the end of this trajectory that small and medium-sized enterprises, and especially large ones, have social and environmental responsibility. Companies, as well as a business-to-business system, evaluate efforts in this area. All this starts with a good feeling. I do not deny to anyone his ethical commitment, I do not deny the progress made, but the truth requires admitting that the text here is not up to the stakes and that some may still have somewhat dubious behaviors and pay for words. To make it simple, one could say that without reporting, one has a blind vision of the company – because it is only short-term, financial and economic – but that CSR, as it exists today, gives a blurred vision, because the documents, illegible by the general public, are not able to tell the truth of the company to our fellow citizens. We believe that today CSR only gives a blurred view of things. If it is still at its stone age, we believe that the new age of CSR must be built. Together with Mr Boris Vallaud, we have moderated a working group which has drawn inspiration from the work of the Collège des Bernardins, at the origin of the texts we are discussing and the idea of a new company. Our reflection suggests that CSR must be based on public principles: public authorities must set the standard. This is even more true in the 21st century, marked by an acceleration of technological-scientific change, and a globalization often without faith or law. Today, private power invades the whole world and conquers standards. There is a kind of privatization of standards. The world of money tells us what a good business or a good life is. For our part, we believe that, in a democracy, it is up to the public authorities to say what a good life and a good business are. This idea of creating a public label doesn't come from nowhere. It comes from the experience of the peasant I am who, together with others, in a cooperative, has adopted a label of organic farming. It is not the milk giant, known to all, that has established organic farming, nor the giant of the big distribution that we all think of, nor a majority or minority union in the agricultural world; It's public power. Thanks to this, there was a contract that brought together all the operators in the sector: producers, processors, consumers... The government has said what organic farming is, it has established a contract that has allowed the private power to deploy, on the principle of freedom and trust, and to prosper: organic farming, which at another time represents 3% of the sector, targets 30% by 2030. She'll have transformed our society. The same applies to the social and solidarity economy. Under the authority of Benoît Hamon, we set up the label "social utility solidarity company": ESUS. In this context, for example, a large cooperative bank could not claim the same savings collection or branding privileges as a SCOP or a co-operative with a reasonable wage scale. Public labels are guarantees for private power and business freedom, not an obstacle to it. In the fragmented and dangerous world in which we live, we believe, without paying for words, that the process of accelerating participation in the common good of enterprises must go through a rise in the power of citizens, and that this rise in power is impossible without a clear and clear indicator. What shape could it take? We can imagine, for example, that a hundred or so criteria would be defined by a debate between Parliament and the public authorities. This would make it possible to establish a classification in three colours: the red company would be very far from the account, the orange company would be on the move, and green companies would have met, for example, two thirds of the 100 criteria. The equality between men and women, which we have just mentioned, the inclusion of disability, the contribution beyond the law to the recycling of waste and the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions, would all be taken into account. In other words, the public authority that sets limits and adopts a law must also be able to recognize, and even, one day, reward, the efforts of an enterprise beyond what the law prescribes. With such a tool, we are no longer in the world of propaganda, which can be that of lies or falsification: we have a landmark given by the public power. The latter allows every citizen to measure according to his/her abilities as consumers, collaborators, or savers, if he/she wants to favour the red enterprise, the orange enterprise or the green enterprise. We bring clarity. All in all, this thought is liberal, in the philosophical and etymological sense of the term. It empowers every citizen to participate in the life of the world, and to emancipate himself. Everything else is lies and propaganda. The new age of CSR is the world of clarity. It does not detract from freedom of enterprise, rather it makes citizens progress in their ability to transform the economy. I think deeply, like all the researchers, all the entrepreneurs, all the trade unionists, and all the NGOs we have spoken to, that we are really dealing with a new frontier. If we can show some boldness with this bill, and not just words, we will not be able to deny my proposal, which is a tool for genuine change. It does not cost anything, and it allows experimentation by the state. We really need this kind of instrument to transform the world and make it habitable.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1155	I have before me several amendments, Nos 1286, 1298 and 1342, which can be presented in a consolidated format. Amendment No 1298 is the subject of two sub-amendments Nos. 1341 and 1340. Mrs Coralie Dubost, rapporteur, has the floor to support them.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1156	At first reading, we adopted an article that was deleted by the Senate and then reinstated in committee, which is intended to provide for a review of existing labels. Mr Potier, we come together to see that CSR labelling has been done in the private sphere for more than fifty years. Today, we find ourselves with a plethora of private organizations and standards. Some are very accessible, others much less. The possibilities of access for businesses are very different, and the readability of these standards is largely subject to caution. The situation is therefore complex for both the entrepreneur and the citizen. That is why we voted for an amendment at first reading so that a review of the existing labels would enable them to be counted, to know their qualities, and to know what types of indicators they were based on. We have re-established this mechanism in committee, but we wanted to go further and make two clarifications in Amendments Nos 1286 and 1298 of the Special Committee, and in Amendment No 1342 as corrected. The first addition concerns the sharing of value. We discussed this at length at first reading and in committee, in particular by dealing with wage savings or pay differentials that we will return to. With regard to value sharing, we want tools that are legible, visible, understandable by all, and educational for companies. We have prepared a device called equiscore, suggested by small companies on the model of nutriscore. A graphic chart allows you to quickly identify whether the value sharing is good, less good, very qualitative or not. The second addition relates to the consistency of labels. I would like to thank Mrs Olivia Gregoire, who has worked so hard to get us to the end of the matter. We don't want to be left with a label review. If, for example, we find that there are 200 CSR labels that use a total of six hundred criteria and indicators, we want the state to determine an indispensable matrix of about fifty or a hundred criteria for a CSR label to be valid.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1157	We got it!
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1158	It must be readable for the benefit of citizens and, above all, businesses. The first reaction of companies that are somewhat familiar with CSR, but are not necessarily large structures with the means to address standardization offices, was to consider it an excellent tool that would help the most virtuous companies. They'll be able to take it right away. We are proposing this solution rather than that of a state public label which seems to us too heavy, too interventionist, and which could put at risk all the advances which have nevertheless taken place thanks to private certifying bodies.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1194	It is now accepted that a recognized foundation of public utility may receive shares or shares of a company with an industrial or commercial activity. But it is also agreed that a foundation with a majority holding a company should not interfere in the management of the latter. However, the foundation must be able to exercise its shareholder rights and to pursue a management policy enabling it to guarantee its resources and thus to be able to carry out its public utility mission. In practice, the legal construction adopted by certain foundations allows them to exercise their shareholder rights without interfering in the management of the company – this is the case for the Pierre Fabre foundation in my department of Tarn. This amendment therefore proposes to apply the provisions of Article 61hA only to situations in which the chosen legal organisation will not be able to ensure that the foundation is not actively involved in the management of the holding company, in order not to penalise foundations that comply with these constraints.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1195	What is the opinion of the committee?

These new provisions should not create difficulties for foundations of public utility which already respect the principle of speciality. However, the law must apply to all. That is why I am asking you to withdraw your amendment to the benefit of the committee, which provides that existing public utility foundations will incorporate the amendments referred to in this Article in the first amendment of their statutes relating to the control of a company.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1202	The objective is to limit the interference of supervisory boards of civil real estate investment companies in investment decisions. The statutes of certain SCPIs provide that, before making investment decisions, the management company must, in accordance with Article L. 214-99 of the Monetary and Financial Code, obtain the prior approval of the Supervisory Board. However, this provision is contrary to European Union law, which has introduced a principle of autonomy for the management of portfolio management companies. The aim of this amendment is therefore to bring our law into line with that of the European Union, and thus to protect investors.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1235	Amendment No 221 by Mr Daniel Fasquelle is being defended.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1236	Mrs Mathilde Panot has the floor to support Amendment No. 728.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1237	It concerns the transparency of pay differentials: this was, I would remind you, a promise of candidate Macron. A minister of the economy had even made statements about the excessive – if not indecent – remuneration of some big bosses. The NGO Oxfam has carried out a major campaign on this issue in order to demand transparency in terms of wage differentials, a transparency to which the French and French are now mostly in favour. Las, what you put in the bill in this respect is an empty shell and Oxfam's spokesperson, Manon Aubry, said, after the Minister of Economy's retro-pedaling: "The government's commitment to transparency on pay differentials was in the end only a vast communication operation that gave birth to a mouse. » Why? Notably because the method you have chosen, that of median and average wages, does not allow you to measure the real differences between low and high wages or their progression. For this reason, Oxfam continues to promote pay transparency and hopes that the gaps will at least be measured by quantile, which would provide truly useful and operational data to measure pay gaps, while at the same time encouraging their reduction. Within La France, we are in favour of imposing constraints on wage differentials. This transparency measure is an extremely important step, which we must take to the end, that is to say, without contenting ourselves with the only median and average wages, in order to make real progress towards the knowledge and reduction of these wage gaps.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1239	I am replying to this amendment and my arguments will then be the same. We have had lengthy debates on this subject at first reading. We are also in the process of transposing a directive by going further than what this transposition would impose on us.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1240	[[Smile]] One more effort!
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1241	The average, median, and executive compensation ratios are forecast to measure these variances. However, our aim is not to create internal conflicts between employees in the company: we are not there to classify wages and employees into categories, but to examine the remuneration of the manager, the average remuneration and the median remuneration. In my view, we are going further than what the directive asks us to do. We have also found a good balance. Moreover, with regard to this amendment in particular, taking into account subsidiaries abroad, this could result mechanically in disproportions that would be wrong and could be instrumentalised. I therefore believe that the balance which was found at first reading, which is a good balance, must be preserved without giving in to haste. Unfavorable opinion.
