The Constitution prevents us from limiting wages, but nothing legally prevents us – this has been verified – from considering that beyond, to be concrete, 220000 to 250,000 euros, remuneration becomes a privilege and that it is no longer a burden for the company. This would lead to a certain decency, assuming that no one can earn in a month more than another earns in a year. On this ethical basis, a sound business economy could be built without subtracting corporate taxes from privileges. This proposal is pragmatic. We have tried to encrypt it, but we miss some information, Madam Secretary of State. However, since in eight days we have not been able to obtain the list of pesticides that France exports, I will certainly not have an immediate answer to my questions. It should be noted that the 1% of French people who receive more than 220000 to 250,000 euros represents a significant increase. The gains obtained through corporate tax would thus be the equivalent of the proceeds of the FATF tax, or even double or triple. It is therefore both a mechanism for the improvement of public life, by imposing decent wages in the enterprise, and a resource for the public authorities, which is in dire need of it, in particular to combat climate change and poverty.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1298	This technical amendment concerns the situation of certain boards of directors. I am, of course, very much in favour of the Copé-Zimmermann law, as well as the principle of nullity of deliberations if this law, on parity on the boards of directors, is not respected. However, I would like to point out the problems encountered in this area by the boards of directors, where appointments are made by list vote or are accompanied by nominations of alternates. In this case, a woman may have a man as an alternate. If his term of office is interrupted before the end of his term, he is therefore the alternate who sits, in which case the board of directors is no longer equal while he was, and therefore the company was in compliance with the law. The same applies to list ballots. Very few companies in France have directors elected by list vote or with an alternate. It would therefore be necessary to await the next election, and the composition of two lists of women and men, or to require all administrators to have a woman as their substitute. This amendment, which is highly technical in nature, aims to solve this problem.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1342	This is the transposition of a European directive which harmonises the liability regime for tour operators. The Directive provides for a simple liability regime, but in transposition the Government has gone so far as to assume full responsibility. It had traditionally existed in French law and was widely referred to by consumer associations. For this reason, we have studied the case law and found that the objective existence of a full liability which cannot be exempted in the event of wrongful conduct may lead to excessive decisions. An example: the guest of a hotel who abused the drinks offered at the bar...
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1343	[[Smile]] Are you talking about Mr. Castaner?
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1344	... and who, for this reason, makes a fall by returning to his room after a walk out of the establishment can attack the hotel in court. That's not acceptable. Many amendments tend to remove this responsibility as a matter of law. For my part, after discussing it with tour operators and consumers, and taking into account both the directive and our legal tradition, I propose a compromise solution: to maintain the words "on its own", but, in return, to provide for an exemption from liability clause for the benefit of tour operators in the event of the customer's wrongdoing. This is not a new legal regime: we have been inspired by a system that already exists for e-commerce professionals. It seems to me that this is a balanced solution.

Unfortunately, there are orange lights, which correspond to points in the right direction, but on which you have not gone far enough. You remove the threshold of 20 employees, but you do not touch the real blocking thresholds, those of 10 and 50 employees. On the social package, you go in the right direction, but you had to remove it completely. You have also maintained the jungle of legal statutes in which business start-ups are struggling, which is a climax for a simplification law for businesses. Our discussions have made some progress on the auditors, but we consider them to be insufficient and in a much too rough time frame. The refusal to create the parliamentary delegation to economic security is a bad thing; Above all, the opposition will not have access to information about economic security, as this denial of democracy is a major shock to us. If, as under the previous mandate, the majority in the National Assembly were the same as that of the Senate, the opposition would be deprived of all access to this information in both chambers. This is absolutely scandalous, and I do not understand your refusal to include the Chairman of the Committee on Finance in your proposal. With respect to the amendment to section 1833 of the Civil Code, I do not understand why you refused the assessment, Mr. Lescure. The obligations of means mentioned by the Secretary of State do place us in the field of civil liability, as well as the normative obligations mentioned by the rapporteur. There are, however, some positive points in this report, which make it difficult to vote against. The Republican Group will therefore adopt a position of abstention on the text.

And perhaps it will be easier to explain to the French what we are doing here, in the Chamber, and what is the meaning of our action, instead of legislating, as we sometimes do, on points of detail. What places our group apart is that twenty-two of us voted the text at the end of its first reading, nine abstained and one voted against. Objectively, we have not changed, in a new reading in the National Assembly, the balance of the text obtained at first reading. I take it that the majority of the UDI-Agir Group will vote for the text and that some of us will abstain, particularly because of the privatisation of Paris airports. Even though we have obtained new guarantees, which I welcome, there is a hard point in the text. Finally, in my personal capacity, I thank you for listening, colleagues, on all benches. I would like to say that we have enjoyed a good working atmosphere. [[Applause on a few benches of the LaREM group]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Madam Chairman of the Special Committee, Madam Secretary of State, I thank you very much for that. ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1470	Mr Philippe Bolo has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1471	The bill can be observed here, but also by the way. From here, it takes the form of long debates and long moments spent together, in committee and in the Chamber. Some topics have taken longer than others, perhaps because they have led us to debate important changes and raise questions about developments that may be perceived differently by each other. Among these long periods of debate that we had – it was normal to have them – are the withdrawal of the State from the capital of Paris Airport, La Française des jeux and Engie. We also mention the definition of the raison d'être of companies, as well as complex subjects. Sometimes, on the contrary, we have dealt with issues that we could ask ourselves how they relate to the companies that everyone here knows. We must also ask ourselves the question of the PACTE law from another perspective: if the press speaks only of the subjects that have occupied the debates, how will the entrepreneurs understand that this law concerns them? And yet, we have legislated on matters that concern them! Several members of the Democratic and Related Group have, like me, devoted a great deal of time to this bill: we have organized meetings with business leaders, workshops that allow them to make their expectations and concerns known to us. This has enabled us to draft amendments, some of which have been accepted. I am thinking, for example, of the need for new financing and the provisions on inter-firm loans. I am also thinking of the involvement of employees in the life of the company: business leaders have told us that they want to divest their company, and we have tabled amendments on the use of the Business Savings Plan to be able to buy shares of their company. I am still thinking of reverse factoring, for those who have reported their difficulties in getting their bills paid. I am thinking of those who had difficulty crossing the thresholds: the five-year deadline will help them. Finally, I am thinking of CSR, and of all those who have told us that they want to adopt a method of production that is closer to the expectations of many citizens. The PACTE bill therefore provides concrete answers to daily questions posed by entrepreneurs and employees. [[Applause on the benches of the LaREM and MODEM groups]] For these reasons, and many others that have been mentioned during the debates, the Group of the Democratic and Related Movement will vote for this draft.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1483	This business growth and transformation plan addresses several issues, because we cannot start a transformation of our businesses, and therefore of our economies, into a few articles. The wealth of this text does not make it a tote, but a text provided and ambitious. [[Exclaims on the banks of the GDR and SOC groups]] This project frees our companies; it helps to set up a business; it protects employees; it allows to share the value created, by promoting participation and interest; It helps our French companies to finance themselves by directing savings through agile and diversified tools. We want companies to engage in a virtuous circle of social and environmental responsibility, and we are therefore changing the social purpose. We are committed to the inclusion of people with disabilities, a struggle so dear to our majority. We strengthen the quality of patents in France, and align our rules on the best European standard. We are strengthening the regulation of Engie and the French game. We are creating a parliamentary delegation for economic security, a tool for monitoring the process of monitoring the strategic investments of the State. It is therefore a strengthening of Parliament's powers of control. [[Smile]] I will not mention here the many other advances in this text – so we too will be freed, and we will be able to return home. One thing is certain: we do not all agree on the privatisation of Paris airports, for reasons that we have developed throughout our discussions. Our discussions have, I believe, informed each other's motivations. But, collectively, we must be certain of one thing: the majority have become aware of the mistakes made in the past. We continue to believe that the role of the state is not to run a mall. On the other hand, it must meet the economic challenges ahead, and France must live up to our ambition. The Innovation and Breakout Fund must be an instrument of our economic strategy. [[Applause on the benches of the LaREM and MODEM groups]] Everything we do, we do it responsibly, humbly, and in the interests of France and the French.

ParlaMint-FR_2019-03-15-O1182.u1485	I too welcome the spirit that reigned over our debates, and the efforts of the Government and the rapporteurs to answer our questions – except in the last part, which has disappointed us. I thought it was better than nothing... But I am extremely upset by communications that I have read on social media: we are doing better than the Senate! It's unheard of. The Government, in connection with the Senate, has removed a provision voted here; you are reintroducing it, of course, but one tenth of its original power. And you'd have to be proud of it? This case will remain, I believe, as a stain on the PACTE law. How can we, six months after the passing of a law, go back to such important and widely voted provisions, keeping his honour? As far as the export of pesticides is concerned, I once again regret that half of the documents we requested eight days ago are still not available. Parliament could not be informed: we still do not know what products are being exported or why these products are being banned in Europe. From a health point of view, from an ethical point of view, from an environmental point of view, this is not a detail! I come to the heart of the bill. Others have said this: privatisations that are being undertaken are faults; and these are not faults like the others because, unlike many other provisions on which another majority can return quite easily, they are irreversible. We are deeply impressed by this: we have failed to convince you to reconsider this decision. Nor do I forget the multiple deregulations on which the oppositions, in all their diversity, have tried to enlighten you. I am not going back to the auditors, nor to the pre-installation training. But I want to tell you that there was more ideology than pragmatism in your decisions. We have not been able to dialogue and convince each other. The big stake for you was the new company! I remember, on behalf of the Socialist Group, reporting a bill in January 2018 on new business and governance; And I remember Erwan Balanant of Laetitia Avia, who told me that my proposals were great, that the majority would go further, that we would all work together. A little over a year later, what happened? Almost nothing. Out of intellectual honesty, I will give you some advanced menus – on the readability of green products, for example. I welcome this progress, which has been achieved thanks in particular to Jean-Noël Barrot. Of course, one more administrator on the boards is better. Of course, there are a few small steps. But overall, what a mismatch between the display and the promise of collaboration, and the result in fine! There are a few small advances, a few small steps, but overall, what a gap between the speech, the display and the promise of a cooperation that you had made us and the result! We must be honest about our inability to reshape the company, given the challenges of the century and the society that is coming. I say this forcefully: we are not on the rendezvous, neither yellow vests, which denounce ill-treated work, nor young people who, still today, spoke of an ill-treated nature. We are not at the rendezvous of this generation of entrepreneurs that I see being born in our territories, in schools that today put forward values other than money as the engine of its ability to undertake, with a profoundly new vision of the world. I have the impression that the decisions we have taken or the cautions we have had are terribly dated. They don't have the modernity you'd like to display, like a fanion. I regret that, in order to reform the company, we have not embarked on a new governance, which would have led us towards the sharing of power within the company, of added value and of knowledge. It is a missed appointment, at a time when we should have drawn here in France the outline of a European design, of a company that distinguishes us from the forms of capitalism that we condemn.
